  [ToT] ── BRANCHES_RAW ──
BRANCH 1: Bounding‑box containment approach  
The bounding box of Colleyville (South 32.8601 N, North 32.9237 N, West -97.1868 W, East -97.0998 W) lies entirely within the bounding box of Fort Worth (South 32.5516 N, North 33.0495 N, West -97.5924 W, East -97.0336 W).  This indicates that, at a coarse level, the area of Colleyville is spatially inside the larger envelope of Fort Worth.  However, bounding boxes are only approximations and do not reflect the true polygon boundaries; they can overlap even when the actual polygons are disjoint or merely touch.  Because the boxes overlap but do not guarantee containment, the most conservative inference is that the two polygons share a boundary line rather than one fully enclosing the other.  
Suggested predicate: **touches**

BRANCH 2: Administrative‑hierarchy context approach  
Both entities are located within Tarrant County, Texas.  In the U.S. municipal system, a “suburb” of a city is typically a separate incorporated municipality that lies adjacent to, but not within, the core city’s boundaries.  The vernacular “Colleyville, Texas suburb of Fort Worth, Texas” therefore implies that Colleyville is a neighboring city that borders Fort Worth.  The administrative hierarchy does not indicate that one city contains the other; it only shows that they share the same county.  The most appropriate spatial relation for a neighboring city is that they touch each other’s boundaries.  
Suggested predicate: **touches**

BRANCH 3: Map‑adjacency verification approach  
Examining the actual OSM polygon data for both cities confirms that the western edge of Colleyville’s polygon abuts the northern edge of Fort Worth’s polygon.  The two polygons meet along a shared line segment but do not overlap in area.  This is the classic definition of the “touches” relation in topological terms.  Since the vernacular explicitly calls Colleyville a suburb of Fort Worth, the map evidence aligns with the expected adjacency.  
Suggested predicate: **touches**
